#### **Religion**

According to [Public Religion Research Institute'](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_Religion_Research_Institute)s 2022 *American Values Survey*, those identifying with a religion or spiritual tradition were approximately 75% of the state's population[.\[132\]](#page-21-9) 69% of Wisconsinites self-identified as [Christian.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian) Specifically, 25% of respondents identified as [Mainline Protestant,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mainline_(Protestant)) 12% as [Evangelical Protestant,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelicalism) 4% as other Protestants, and 27% as [Catholic.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic) Roughly 25% of the population were unaffiliated with any religious body. Small minorities of [Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judaism) (1%), [Hindus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism) (1%), [Buddhists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhism) (1%), [Jehovah's Witnesses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jehovah%27s_Witnesses) (1%), [Unitarian Universalists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unitarian_Universalism) (1%), [Muslims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam) (<1%), [Mormons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mormonism) (<1%), and other faiths exist according to this study[.\[131\]](#page-21-10)

Christianity is the predominant religion in Wisconsin. Per the [Association of Religion Data Archives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_of_Religion_Data_Archives)' 2020 study, Catholicism was the single-largest denomination with 1,237,342 adherents, followed by the [Evangelical Lutheran Church in America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelical_Lutheran_Church_in_America) with 316,245 members and the [Wisconsin Evangelical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_Evangelical_Lutheran_Synod)

[Lutheran Synod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_Evangelical_Lutheran_Synod) with 209,788 adherents[.\[133\]](#page-21-11) 276,904 adhered to [nondenominational](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nondenominational_Christianity) [Christianity.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nondenominational_Christianity)

#### **Crime**

Statewide FBI Crime statistics for 2009 include 144 murders/non-negligent manslaughter; 1,108 rapes; 4,850 robberies; 8,431 aggravated assaults; and 147,486 property crimes.[\[134\]](#page-21-12) Wisconsin also publishes its own statistics through the Bureau of Justice Information and Analysis.[\[135\]](#page-21-13) The state reported 14,603 violent crimes in 2009, with a clearance rate (% solved) of 50%.[\[136\]](#page-21-14) The state reported 4,633 sexual assaults in 2009, with an overall clearance rate for sexual assaults of 57%.

## **Economy**

In 2023, Wisconsin's [gross state product](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gross_state_product) was \$413.966 billion, making it [21st among U. S.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U. S._states_and_territories_by_GDP) [states.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U. S._states_and_territories_by_GDP)[\[137\]](#page-21-15) The economy of Wisconsin is driven by manufacturing, agriculture, and tourism. The [per capita personal income](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Per_capita_personal_income_in_the_United_States) was \$61,475 in 2022

## ![](_page_8_Figure_7.jpeg)

and was \$66,596 when adjusted by regional price parity. In 2024, the state's seasonally adjusted unemployment rate has hovered at 3.0%, lower than the U. S. average[.\[138\]](#page-21-16) Since 2009, Wisconsin's [minimum wage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minimum_wage) has been \$7.25, the same as the federal rate[.\[139\]](#page-21-17)

Eight corporations based in Wisconsin are listed on the *[Fortune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortune_500)* [500.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortune_500) In 2024, the list included [Northwestern Mutual,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwestern_Mutual) [Fiserv,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fiserv) [Manpower Group,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manpower Group) [Rockwell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rockwell_Automation) [Automation,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rockwell_Automation) and [WEC Energy Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WEC_Energy_Group) (all based in Milwaukee), as well as [Kohl's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kohl%27s) (based in [Menomonee Falls\)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menomonee_Falls,_Wisconsin), [American Family Insurance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Family_Insurance) (based in Madison), and [Oshkosh Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oshkosh_Corporation) (based in [Oshkosh\)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oshkosh,_Wisconsin).[\[140\]](#page-21-18)

### **Manufacturing**

As of 2015, the number of manufacturing jobs in the state was approximately 500,000, similar to the figure in 1970. However, it declined as a share of the state's gross state product between 2000 and 2016 from about 21.5% to 18.5%, a proportion that is eighth among all states[.\[141\]](#page-21-19) Similarly, as a share of total employment, manufacturing declined from 28% in 1970 to 14% in 2015. The state's economic output from manufacturing was \$48.9 billion in 2008, making it the tenth largest among states in manufacturing gross domestic product.[\[142\]](#page-21-20)

Major Wisconsin companies in manufacturing include the [Kohler Company;](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kohler_Company) [Mercury Marine;](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercury_Marine) [Rockwell Automation;](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rockwell_Automation) [Johnson Controls;](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johnson_Controls) [John Deere;](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Deere) [Briggs & Stratton;](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Briggs_%26_Stratton) [Milwaukee Electric Tool Company;](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milwaukee_Electric_Tool_Company) [Miller Electric;](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miller_Electric) [Caterpillar Inc.;](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caterpillar_Inc.) [Joy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joy_Global) [Global;](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joy_Global) [Oshkosh Corporation;](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oshkosh_Corporation) [Harley-Davidson;](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harley-Davidson) [Case IH;](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Case_IH) [S. C. Johnson & Son;](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S._C._Johnson_%26_Son) [Ashley Furniture;](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashley_Furniture) [Ariens;](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ariens) and [Evinrude](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evinrude_Outboard_Motors) [Outboard Motors.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evinrude_Outboard_Motors)

Wisconsin has a significant lumber industry, being a major producer of paper and packaging. Wisconsin ranks first nationwide in the production of paper products; the lower Fox River from [Lake Winnebago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Winnebago) to [Green Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Bay_(Lake_Michigan)) has 24 [paper mills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paper_mill) along its 39 miles (63 km) stretch.[\[143\]](#page-21-21)

A large part of the state's manufacturing sector includes commercial food processing, including well-known brands such as [Oscar Mayer,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oscar_Mayer) [Tombstone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tombstone_(pizza)) frozen pizza, [Johnsonville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johnsonville_Foods) [brats,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bratwurst) and [Usinger's sausage.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fred_Usinger) [Kraft Foods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kraft_Foods) alone employs more than 5,000 people in the state. Milwaukee is a major producer of beer and was formerly headquarters for [Miller Brewing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miller_Brewing_Company) [Company—](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miller_Brewing_Company)the nation's second-largest brewer—until it merged with Coors. Formerly, [Schlitz,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Schlitz_Brewing_Company) [Blatz,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valentin_Blatz_Brewing_Company) and [Pabst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pabst_Brewing_Company) were cornerstone breweries in Milwaukee.

The development and manufacture of healthcare devices and software is a growing sector of the state's economy, with key players such as [GE Healthcare,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GE_Healthcare) [Epic Systems,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epic_Systems) and [Tomo Therapy.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tomo Therapy)

## ![](_page_8_Picture_16.jpeg)

The [U. S. Bank Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U. S._Bank_Center_(Milwaukee)) in [downtown Milwaukee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_Milwaukee) is home to the main headquarters of [Baird,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baird_(investment_bank)) [Foley & Lardner,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foley_%26_Lardner) and [Sensient Technologies,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sensient_Technologies) as well as regional headquarters for [U. S.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U. S._Bank) [Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U. S._Bank) and [IBM.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IBM)

#### **Agriculture**

Wisconsin produces about a quarter of America's cheese, leading the nation in cheese production.[\[144\]](#page-22-0)[\[145\]](#page-22-1) It is second in milk production, after [California,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California) [\[146\]](#page-22-2) and third in per-capita milk production, behind California and [Vermont.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vermont)[\[147\]](#page-22-3) Wisconsin is second in butter production, producing about one-quarter of the nation's butter.[\[148\]](#page-22-4) Wisconsin requires cheese and butter makers to hold a license for production, being the only state in the US to require certification for either[.\[149\]](#page-22-5)

The state ranks first nationally in the production of corn for [silage,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silage) [cranberries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cranberries)[,\[150\]](#page-22-6) [ginseng,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ginseng)[\[151\]](#page-22-7) and [snap beans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snap_beans) for processing. It grows more than half the national crop of cranberries[.\[150\]](#page-22-6) and 97% of the nation's ginseng[.\[151\]](#page-22-7) Wisconsin is also a leading producer of [oats,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oat) potatoes, carrots, tart [cherries,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cherries) [maple syrup,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maple_syrup) and [sweet corn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sweet_corn) for processing.

The significance of the state's agricultural production is exemplified by the depiction of a Holstein cow, an ear of corn, and a wheel of cheese on Wisconsin's [state quarter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_quarter) design[.\[152\]](#page-22-8) The state annually selects an ["Alice in](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alice_in_Dairyland) [Dairyland"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alice_in_Dairyland) to promote the state's agricultural products around the world[.\[153\]](#page-22-9) The prominence of the dairy industry in Wisconsin has led to Wisconsin being known as "America's Dairyland"[,\[154\]](#page-22-10)[\[2\]](#page-15-1) which was made the official state slogan in 1940[.\[155\]](#page-22-11)

## ![](_page_9_Picture_5.jpeg)

Dairy cows at a [Wisconsin dairy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_dairy_industry) [farm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_dairy_industry)

#### **Tourism**

According to the Department of Tourism, tourism in Wisconsin generated \$20.9 billion in total economic impact in 2021 and is the state's third-largest sector[.\[156\]](#page-22-12) Festivals such as [Summerfest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Summerfest) and the [EAA Air Venture Oshkosh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EAA_Air Venture_Oshkosh) draw international attention, along with hundreds of thousands of visitors annually[.\[157\]](#page-22-13) Notable attractions across the state include the [Harley-Davidson Museum,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harley-Davidson_Museum) [Lambeau Field,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lambeau_Field) [Milwaukee Art Museum,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milwaukee_Art_Museum) [National Railroad Museum,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Railroad_Museum) and [Wisconsin State Capital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_State_Capital)[.\[158\]](#page-22-14) Other tourist destinations include [Taliesin,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taliesin_(studio)) the studio of architect [Frank Lloyd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_Lloyd_Wright) [Wright;](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_Lloyd_Wright) [House on the Rock,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_on_the_Rock) a complex of architecturally distinct rooms; and the [Circus World Museum,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circus_World_Museum) located in the [Ringling brothers'](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ringling_brothers) hometown.

The [Dells of the Wisconsin River,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dells_of_the_Wisconsin_River) a gorge noted for its rock formations in south-central Wisconsin, annually attracts more than four million visitors between water tours of the scenery and numerous [theme parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theme_park) in the region.[\[156\]](#page-22-12) Nearby [Devil's Lake State Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Devil%27s_Lake_State_Park_(Wisconsin)) is one of the most visited of [Wisconsin's state parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Wisconsin_state_parks) due to its proximity to the Dells and its own scenery.

## ![](_page_9_Picture_10.jpeg)

The [Dells of the Wisconsin River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dells_of_the_Wisconsin_River) form a tourism region focused on river features and nearby [theme parks.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theme_park)

The [Door Peninsula,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Door_Peninsula) which extends off the eastern coast of the state, contains [Door County,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Door_County,_Wisconsin) a popular destination for boaters due to the large number of natural harbors and boat launches on both the Green Bay and Lake Michigan sides of the peninsula. The area draws more than two million visitors yearly to its quaint villages, seasonal cherry picking, and fi[sh boils.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fish_boil)[\[159\]](#page-22-15)[\[160\]](#page-22-16)

Given the large number of lakes and rivers in the state, water recreation is popular. In the Northwoods Lake Country, what had been an industrial area focused on timber has largely been transformed into a vacation destination. Popular interest in the environment and environmentalism, added to traditional interests in hunting and fishing, has attracted a large urban audience within driving range[.\[161\]](#page-22-17) [Lake Geneva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Geneva,_Wisconsin) in southeastern Wisconsin is similarly popular for water recreation.

#### **Energy**

Wisconsin has no production of oil, gas, or coal.[\[162\]](#page-22-18) Its in-state electrical generation is mostly from coal. Other important electricity sources are natural gas and nuclear.[\[162\]](#page-22-18)

The state has a mandate that ten percent of its electrical energy come from renewable sources by the end of 2015[.\[163\]](#page-22-19) This goal has been met, but not with in-state sources. As of 2014, a third of that ten percent comes from out-of-state sources, mostly wind-generated electricity from Minnesota and Iowa. The state has agnostic policies for developing wind power in state[.\[164\]](#page-22-20)

#### **Taxation**

Wisconsin collects personal [income taxes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Income_tax) which range from 4% to 7.65% based on five [income brackets.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Income_bracket) The state [sales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sales_tax) and [use tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Use_tax) rate is 5.0%. Fifty-nine counties have an additional sales/use tax of 0.5%.[\[165\]](#page-22-21)

The most common property tax assessed on Wisconsin residents is the real [property tax,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Property_tax) or their residential property tax. Wisconsin does not impose a property tax on vehicles, but does levy an annual registration fee. Property taxes are the most important tax revenue source for Wisconsin's local governments, as well as major methods of funding school districts, vocational-technical colleges, special purpose districts and tax incremental finance districts. Equalized values are based on the full market value of all taxable property in the state, except for agricultural land. To provide property tax relief for farmers, the value of agricultural land is determined by its value for agricultural uses, rather than for its possible development value. Equalized values are used to distribute state aid payments to counties, municipalities, and technical colleges. Assessments prepared by local assessors are used to distribute the property tax burden within individual municipalities.

## ![](_page_9_Figure_20.jpeg)

## Wisconsin Budget (2021)

Wisconsin does not assess a tax on [intangible property.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intangible_property) Wisconsin does not collect [inheritance taxes.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inheritance_tax) Until January 1, 2008, Wisconsin's [estate tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estate_tax) was decoupled from the federal estate tax laws; therefore the state imposed its own estate tax on certain large estates.[\[166\]](#page-22-22)

There are no [toll roads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toll_road) in Wisconsin; highway construction and maintenance are funded in part by [motor fuel tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motor_fuel_tax) revenues, and the remaining balance is drawn from the State General Fund. Non-highway road construction and maintenance are funded by local governments (municipalities or counties).

## **Culture**

Residents of Wisconsin are referred to as Wisconsinites. The traditional prominence of references to [dairy farming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dairy_farming) and [cheesemaking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheesemaking) in Wisconsin's rural economy (the state's [license plates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/License_plates) have read "America's Dairyland" since 1940)[\[167\]](#page-23-0) have led to the nickname (sometimes used pejoratively among non-residents) of ["cheeseheads"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheesehead), and to the creation of "cheesehead hats" made of yellow foam in the shape of a wedge of cheese.

Numerous [ethnic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic) festivals are held throughout Wisconsin to celebrate the heritage of its citizens. Such festivals include [Summerfest,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Summerfest) [Oktoberfest,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oktoberfest_celebrations) [Polish Fest,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_Fest) [Festa Italiana,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Festa_Italiana) [Irish Fest,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irish_Fest) Bastille Days, [Syttende Mai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syttende_Mai) (Norwegian Constitution Day), Brat(wurst) Days in [Sheboygan,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheboygan,_Wisconsin) [Polka Days,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pulaski,_Wisconsin#Pulaski_Polka_Days) Cheese Days in [Monroe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monroe,_Wisconsin) and [Mequon,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mequon) African World Festival, Indian Summer, Arab Fest, Wisconsin Highland Games, and many others.[\[168\]](#page-23-1)

## ![](_page_10_Picture_6.jpeg)

[Cheesehead](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheesehead) hats at the [2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Summer_Olympics) [Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Summer_Olympics)

#### **Architecture**

With the immigration of [northern Europeans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nordic_and_Scandinavian_Americans) into Wisconsin and the [upper Midwest,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_Midwest) they brought the techniques of building [log homes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Log_house) with them.[\[169\]](#page-23-2)

The [Milwaukee Art Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milwaukee_Art_Museum) is known for its Quadracci Pavilion created by Spanish architect [Santiago Calatrava.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santiago_Calatrava) The Quadracci Pavilion contains a movable, wing-like Burke *[brise soleil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brise_soleil)* that opens up for a wingspan of 217 feet (66 m) during the day, folding over the tall, arched structure at night or during inclement weather.[\[170\]](#page-23-3)

[Frank Lloyd Wright,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_Lloyd_Wright) an architect known as the pioneer of [Prairie School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prairie_School) architecture and the [Usonian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Usonia) home concept, was raised in Wisconsin. Wright's home and studio in the 20th century was at [Taliesin,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taliesin_(studio)) south of [Spring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spring_Green,_Wisconsin) [Green, Wisconsin.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spring_Green,_Wisconsin) Taliesin and the Usonian [Jacobs I House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herbert_and_Katherine_Jacobs_First_House) in Madison are listed as [UNESCO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO) [World Heritage Sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Heritage_Site) as part of ["The 20th-Century Architecture of Frank Lloyd Wright"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_20th-Century_Architecture_of_Frank_Lloyd_Wright).[\[25\]](#page-16-12) Other notable works of Wright in Wisconsin include the [Annunciation Greek Orthodox Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annunciation_Greek_Orthodox_Church) in Wauwatosa, [First Unitarian Society of Madison,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Unitarian_Society_of_Madison) and [Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johnson_Wax_Headquarters)

## ![](_page_10_Picture_12.jpeg)

[Taliesin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taliesin_(studio)) was the studio of architect [Frank Lloyd Wright.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_Lloyd_Wright)

[Wax Headquarters.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johnson_Wax_Headquarters) [Monona Terrace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monona_Terrace) in Madison, a convention center designed by Taliesin architect Anthony Puttnam, is based on a 1930s design by Wright[.\[171\]](#page-23-4)

#### **Alcohol**

Drinking has long been considered a significant part of Wisconsin culture, and the state ranks at or near the top of national measures of per-capita alcohol consumption, consumption of alcohol per state, and proportion of drinkers. Consumption per-capita per-event, however, ranks low among the nation; number of events (number of times alcohol is involved) is significantly higher or highest, but consumption at each event smaller, marking Wisconsin's consumption as frequent and moderate[.\[172\]](#page-23-5) Factors such as cultural identification with the state's heritage of German immigration, the long-standing presence of major breweries in Milwaukee, and a cold climate are often associated with the prevalence of drinking in Wisconsin.

## ![](_page_10_Picture_17.jpeg)

The [Miller Brewery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miller_Brewing_Company) in Milwaukee

Many large breweries were founded in Wisconsin, largely in Milwaukee, which gained the epithet "Brew City" before the turn of the century. [Miller Brewing Company,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miller_Brewing_Company) [Pabst Brewing Company,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pabst_Brewing_Company) [Joseph Schlitz Brewing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Schlitz_Brewing_Company) [Company,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Schlitz_Brewing_Company) and [Jacob Leinenkugel Brewing Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacob_Leinenkugel_Brewing_Company) all began as local businesses before entering national markets. Several other popular craft brews include Ale Asylum,[\[173\]](#page-23-6) [Capital,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_Brewery) [Sprecher,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sprecher_Brewery) and [New Glarus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Glarus_Brewing_Company), the latter being well known for the Spotted Cow Farmhouse Ale only sold in Wisconsin[.\[174\]](#page-23-7)

In Wisconsin, the legal drinking age is 21, except when accompanied by a parent, guardian, or spouse who is at least 21 years old. Age requirements are waived for possessing alcohol when employed by a brewer, brewpub, wholesaler, or producer of alcohol fuel. The minimum legal age to purchase alcohol is 21, with no exceptions.[\[175\]](#page-23-8) The Absolute Sobriety law states that any person not of legal drinking age (currently 21) may not drive after consuming alcohol[.\[176\]](#page-23-9) DUI offenses were lowered to BAC 0.08 in 2003 as a result of federal government pressure[.\[177\]](#page-23-10)

#### **Cuisine**

Wisconsin's cuisine is famous for [its cheese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_cheese) as well as other dairy products. [Colby cheese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colby_cheese) was first created in Wisconsin in 1885 (named after [Colby,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colby,_Wisconsin) [Wisconsin\)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colby,_Wisconsin), and [brick cheese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brick_cheese) was first created in the state in 1877[.\[178\]](#page-23-11)[\[179\]](#page-23-12) The state is the only producer of [Limburger cheese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Limburger_cheese) in the United States. [Cheese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheese_curd) [curds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheese_curd) are a popular variant that can be eaten separately cold as a snack, or covered in batter and fried as an appetizer, often served with [ranch dressing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ranch_dressing) as a dipping sauce. [Hot and spicy cheese bread](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hot_and_spicy_cheese_bread) is a popular type of bread created and sold in Madison.

The state is the nation's top producer and consumer of [bratwurst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bratwurst) and is also a major producer and consumer of [summer sausage.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Summer_sausage) Wisconsin is the country's leading producer of [cranberries,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cranberries) which is also the state's official fruit.[\[180\]](#page-23-13) [Dane County Farmers' Market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dane_County_Farmers%27_Market) in Madison is the largest producers-only farmer's market in the nation[.\[181\]](#page-23-14)[\[182\]](#page-23-15)

The Friday night fi[sh fry,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fish_fry) often battered and fried [perch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perch) or [walleye,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walleye) is traditional throughout Wisconsin, while in northeast Wisconsin and [Door County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Door_County,_Wisconsin) the fi[sh boil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fish_boil) is more popular. The [supper club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supper_club) is another common phenomenon of Wisconsin culinary heritage and often a destination for fish frys; other fried food are common side dishes, along with condiments of tartar sauce and [cole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cole_slaw) [slaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cole_slaw) or crimson slaw, a variety of cole slaw that incorporates Wisconsin cranberries[.\[183\]](#page-23-16) [Beer cheese soup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beer_soup) is usually made from a variety of beer and cheddar or Colby cheese with sausage, potatoes, and green onions.

[Booyah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Booyah_(stew)) is a [stew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stew) common to Wisconsin, commonly using meat and a mirepoix of vegetables cooked together in a "booyah kettle" over low heat for several days[.\[184\]](#page-23-17)

The southeastern city of [Racine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racine,_Wisconsin) is known for its [kringle,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kringle) a sweet flaky pastry often served as a dessert. The recipe was brought by [Danish immigrants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danish_Americans) to the region in the 1800s and became the official state pastry of Wisconsin in 2013.[\[185\]](#page-23-18)[\[186\]](#page-23-19) The [Wisconsin State Fair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_State_Fair) is known for its giant [cream pu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cream_puff)ffs[.\[187\]](#page-23-20)[\[188\]](#page-23-21)

## ![](_page_11_Picture_5.jpeg)

Fried [cheese curds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheese_curd)

The [butter burger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butter_burger) originated in Wisconsin, most likely in [Solly's Grille](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solly%27s_Grille) in [Glendale.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glendale,_Wisconsin)[\[189\]](#page-23-22) [Culver's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culver%27s) is a midwestern [fast casual food restaurant chain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fast_casual_restaurant) originally from [Sauk City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sauk_City,_Wisconsin) and currently headquartered in [Prairie du Sac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prairie_du_Sac,_Wisconsin) known for serving butter burgers, fried cheese curds, and frozen custard. [La Croix Sparkling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Croix_Sparkling_Water) [Water](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Croix_Sparkling_Water) originates from [La Crosse.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Crosse,_Wisconsin)

#### **Music**

Wisconsin's music festivals include Eaux Claires[,\[190\]](#page-24-0) [Country Fest,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Country_Fest) Country Jam USA, the [Hodag Country Festival,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hodag_Country_Festival) [Lifest,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lifest) Porterfield Country Music Festival, Country Thunder USA in Twin Lakes[,\[190\]](#page-24-0) and Country USA. Milwaukee hosts [Summerfest,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Summerfest) dubbed "The World's Largest Music Festival", every year. This festival is held at the lakefront [Henry Maier Festival Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Maier_Festival_Park) just south of downtown, as are a summer-long array of [ethnic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic) musical festivals. The [Wisconsin Area Music Industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_Area_Music_Industry) provides an annual WAMI event where it presents an awards show for top Wisconsin artists.[\[191\]](#page-24-1)

## ![](_page_11_Picture_10.jpeg)

[American Family Insurance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Family_Insurance_Amphitheater) [Amphitheater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Family_Insurance_Amphitheater) is the largest venue at the annual [Summerfest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Summerfest) music festival in Milwaukee.

#### **Recreation**

The varied landscape of Wisconsin makes the state a popular vacation destination for outdoor recreation. Winter events include skiing, ice fishing and [snowmobile derbies.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Championship_Snowmobile_Derby) Wisconsin is situated on two Great Lakes and has many inland lakes of varied size; the state contains 11,188 square miles (28,980 km2) of water, more than all but three other states[—Alaska,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska) Michigan, and Florida.[\[192\]](#page-24-2) The [Wisconsin Shipwreck Coast National Marine Sanctuary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_Shipwreck_Coast_National_Marine_Sanctuary) was established in 2021 in the waters of Lake Michigan off Wisconsin and is the site of a large number of historically significant [shipwrecks.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shipwreck)[\[193\]](#page-24-3)[\[194\]](#page-24-4)[\[195\]](#page-24-5)

Outdoor activities are popular in Wisconsin, especially hunting and fishing. One of the most prevalent game animals is the [whitetail deer.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whitetail_deer) Each year in

Wisconsin, well over 600,000 deer-hunting licenses are sold.[\[196\]](#page-24-6) In 2008, the [Wisconsin Department of Natural Resources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_Department_of_Natural_Resources) projected the pre-hunt deer population to be between 1.5 and 1.7 million.

#### **Sports**

Wisconsin is represented by major league teams in three sports: football, baseball, and basketball. [Lambeau Field,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lambeau_Field) located in [Green Bay, Wisconsin,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Bay,_Wisconsin) is home to the [National Football League'](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Football_League)s [Green Bay Packers.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Bay_Packers) The Packers have been part of the NFL since the league's second season in 1921 and are the smallest city franchise in the NFL, and the only one owned by shareholders statewide.[\[197\]](#page-24-7) The [Milwaukee Brewers,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milwaukee_Brewers) the state's only major league baseball team, have played in [American Family Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Family_Field) in Milwaukee since 2001. Before the Brewers, Milwaukee had two prior Major League teams. The first team, also called the Brewers, played only one season in 1901 before becoming the [St. Louis Browns.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Louis_Browns) Milwaukee was also the home of the now[-Atlanta Braves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlanta_Braves) franchise when they moved from Boston from 1953 to 1965, winning the [World Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Series) in 1957.[\[198\]](#page-24-8) The [Milwaukee Bucks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milwaukee_Bucks) of the [National Basketball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Basketball_Association) [Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Basketball_Association) play home games at the [Fiserv Forum.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fiserv_Forum)[\[199\]](#page-24-9)

The state also has minor league teams in hockey [\(Milwaukee Admirals\)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milwaukee_Admirals) and baseball (the [Wisconsin Timber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_Timber_Rattlers) [Rattlers,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_Timber_Rattlers) based in [Appleton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Appleton,_Wisconsin) and the [Beloit Sky Carp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beloit_Sky_Carp) of the [High-A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High-A) minor leagues). In addition to these affiliated minor league teams, Wisconsin has two [American Association of Professional Baseball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Association_of_Professional_Baseball) teams, being the [Milwaukee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milwaukee_Milkmen) [Milkmen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milwaukee_Milkmen) based in [Franklin,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franklin,_Milwaukee_County,_Wisconsin)[\[200\]](#page-24-10) and the [Lake Country Dock Hounds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Country_Dock Hounds) based in [Oconomowoc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oconomowoc,_Wisconsin)[.\[201\]](#page-24-11) Wisconsin is also home to nine [Northwoods League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwoods_League) teams. The [Madison Mallards,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madison_Mallards) the [La Crosse Loggers,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Crosse_Loggers) the [Lakeshore Chinooks,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lakeshore_Chinooks) the [Eau Claire Express,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eau_Claire_Express) the [Fond du Lac Dock Spiders,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fond_du_Lac_Dock_Spiders) the [Green Bay Rockers,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Bay_Rockers) the [Kenosha King](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kenosha_Kingfish)fish, the [Wausau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wausau_Woodchucks) [Woodchucks,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wausau_Woodchucks) and the [Wisconsin Rapids Rafters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_Rapids_Rafters) all play in a collegiate all-star summer league. In addition to the Packers, Green Bay is also the home to an [indoor football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indoor_American_football) team, the [Green Bay Blizzard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Bay_Blizzard) of the [IFL.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indoor_Football_League) The state is home to the seven-time MISL/MASL Champion [Milwaukee Wave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milwaukee_Wave)[.\[202\]](#page-24-12) Wisconsin is also home to [Forward](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forward_Madison_FC) [Madison FC,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forward_Madison_FC) which is a professional soccer team that plays in the [USL League One.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USL_League_One) The Northern Elite Football League consists of many amateur semi-pro teams from Wisconsin.

[Lambeau Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lambeau_Field) is home to the publicly owned [Green Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Bay_Packers) [Packers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Bay_Packers) of the [National Football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Football_League) [League.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Football_League)

## ![](_page_11_Figure_21.jpeg)

[Road America,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Road_America) near [Elkhart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elkhart_Lake,_Wisconsin) [Lake,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elkhart_Lake,_Wisconsin) has hosted [motorsport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motorsport) races since the 1950s.

Wisconsin also has many [college sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_sports) programs, including the [NCAA Division I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NCAA_Division_I) [Wisconsin Badgers,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_Badgers) [Milwaukee Panthers,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milwaukee_Panthers) and [Green Bay Phoenix.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Bay_Phoenix) The [Marquette Golden Eagles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marquette_Golden_Eagles) of the [Big East Conference,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_East_Conference_(1979%E2%80%932013)) the state's other major collegiate program, is known for its [men's basketball team.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marquette_Golden_Eagles_men%27s_basketball) Many other schools in the University of Wisconsin system compete in the [Wisconsin Intercollegiate Athletic Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_Intercollegiate_Athletic_Conference) at the [Division III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Division_III_(NCAA)) level. The conference is one of the most successful in the nation, claiming 107 NCAA national championships in 15 different sports as of March 30, 2015[.\[203\]](#page-24-13)

The [Milwaukee Mile,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milwaukee_Mile) an oval track opened in 1903, is the oldest operating motorsports venue in the world, having hosted the [Indy Car Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indy Car_Series) and [NASCAR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NASCAR_Xfinity_Series) Xfi[nity Series.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NASCAR_Xfinity_Series)[\[204\]](#page-24-14) [Road America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Road_America) near [Elkhart Lake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elkhart_Lake,_Wisconsin) hosts races in the [Indy Car Series,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indy Car_Series) [IMSA Sports Car Championship,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IMSA_Sports Car_Championship) [Sports Car Club of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_Car_Club_of_America) [GT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GT_World_Challenge_America) [World Challenge America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GT_World_Challenge_America) and [Trans-Am Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trans-Am_Series) and the [Moto America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moto America) Superbike Championship as of 2024. The [World Championship Snowmobile Derby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Championship_Snowmobile_Derby) is held at [Eagle River, Wisconsin.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eagle_River,_Wisconsin) The world championship off[-road racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Off-road_racing) event is held at [Crandon International O](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crandon_International_Off-Road_Raceway)ff-Road Raceway.

Wisconsin is home to the nation's oldest operating [velodrome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Velodrome) in [Kenosha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kenosha) where races have been held every year since 1927.[\[205\]](#page-24-15)

[Sheboygan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheboygan,_Wisconsin) is home to [Whistling Straits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whistling_Straits) golf club which has hosted [PGA Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PGA_Championship) in 2004, 2010 and 2015 and the [Ryder Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ryder_Cup) golf competition between USA and Europe in 2020[.\[206\]](#page-24-16) The [Greater Milwaukee Open,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Milwaukee_Open) later named the [U. S. Bank Championship in Milwaukee,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U. S._Bank_Championship_in_Milwaukee) was a PGA Tour tournament from 1968 to 2009 held annually in [Brown Deer.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brown_Deer) In 2017, [Erin Hills,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erin_Hills) a golf course in [Erin, Wisconsin,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erin,_Wisconsin) approximately 30 miles northwest of Milwaukee, hosted the [U. S. Open.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U. S._Open_(golf))[\[207\]](#page-24-17)

## **Government and politics**

The [Constitution of Wisconsin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Wisconsin) outlines the structure and function of state government, which is organized into three branches: executive, legislative, and judicial. The [Wisconsin Blue Book](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_Blue_Book) is the primary published reference about the government and politics of the state. Re-published every two years, copies are available from state legislators.[\[208\]](#page-24-18) The law of the [Menominee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menominee) also applies within the [Menominee Indian Reservation.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menominee_Indian_Reservation)

#### **State government**

The executive branch is headed by the [Governor of Wisconsin.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor_of_Wisconsin) The current governor, [Tony Evers,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tony_Evers) assumed office on January 7, 2019. The Wisconsin Constitution grants the governor a veto on bills passed by the state legislature as well as a [line-item veto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Line-item_veto) on appropriation bills. A [lieutenant governor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lieutenant_Governor_of_Wisconsin) succeeds the governor in the event of any removal from office and performs any duties assigned by the governor. The current lieutenant governor is [Sara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sara_Rodriguez) [Rodriguez.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sara_Rodriguez) The other elected constitutional offices in the executive branch are the [secretary of state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secretary_of_State_of_Wisconsin) [\(Sarah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarah_Godlewski) [Godlewski\)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarah_Godlewski), [treasurer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Treasurer_of_Wisconsin) [\(John Leiber\)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Leiber), [attorney general](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attorney_General_of_Wisconsin) [\(Josh Kaul\)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josh_Kaul), and the non-partisan [superintendent of public](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superintendent_of_Public_Instruction_of_Wisconsin) [instruction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superintendent_of_Public_Instruction_of_Wisconsin) [\(Jill Underly\)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jill_Underly).[\[209\]](#page-24-19)[\[210\]](#page-25-0)

## ![](_page_12_Picture_10.jpeg)

The [Wisconsin State Capitol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_State_Capitol) is located on the [isthmus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isthmus) between [Lake Mendota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Mendota) and [Lake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Monona) [Monona,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Monona) in the city of [Madison.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madison,_Wisconsin)

The [Wisconsin State Legislature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_State_Legislature) is Wisconsin's legislative branch. The Legislature is a [bicameral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bicameral) body consisting of the [Wisconsin State Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_State_Assembly) and the [Wisconsin State Senate.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_State_Senate) The Assembly has 99 members, and the Senate has 33 members. All 99 members of the Wisconsin Assembly are elected in a two-year term cycle without [term limits.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Term_limits_in_the_United_States) Similarly, all 33 members of the Wisconsin Senate are elected in a four-year cycle, also without term limits[.\[211\]](#page-25-1) Half of the Senate is elected every two years. Members of both houses of the Legislature vote within their ranks to select presiding officers, such as the [Speaker of the Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speaker_(politics)) and the [President of the Senate.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_the_Senate) Legislators in both the Senate and the Assembly receive an annual salary of \$55,141. Over two years, each legislator is allotted \$66,008 to cover general office expenses, printing, postage and district mailings[.\[212\]](#page-25-2)

## ![](_page_12_Picture_13.jpeg)

The [Senate Chamber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_State_Senate) of the Wisconsin State Capitol

Wisconsin's court system has four levels: municipal courts, circuit courts, the Court of Appeals, and the Supreme Court. Municipal courts typically handle cases involving [local ordinance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_ordinance) matters. The [circuit courts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_Circuit_Court) are Wisconsin's

[trial courts,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trial_courts) they have [original jurisdiction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Original_jurisdiction) in all civil and criminal cases within the state. Challenges to circuit court rulings are heard by the [Wisconsin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_Court_of_Appeals) [Court of Appeals,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_Court_of_Appeals) consisting of sixteen judges who typically sit in three-judge panels. As the state's highest appellate court, the [Wisconsin Supreme Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_Supreme_Court) may hear both appeals from lower courts and original actions. In addition to deciding cases, the Supreme Court is responsible for administering the state's court system and regulating the practice of law in Wisconsin[.\[213\]](#page-25-3)

#### **Federal representation**

Following the 2020 census [reapportionment,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_congressional_apportionment) Wisconsin has eight seats in the U. S. House of Representatives. As of the [119th United States Congress,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/119th_United_States_Congress) six federal representatives are Republicans while two are Democrats. [Gwen Moore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gwen_Moore) (D[-04\)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin%27s_4th_congressional_district) is the most senior member of the Wisconsin delegation to the U. S. House of Representatives. Wisconsin's [senior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seniority_in_the_United_States_Senate) [U. S. senator,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Senator) [Ron Johnson,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ron_Johnson) is a Republican, while its junior, [Tammy Baldwin,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tammy_Baldwin) is a Democrat.[\[214\]](#page-25-4)

Wisconsin is under the [appellate jurisdiction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Appellate_jurisdiction) of the [United States Court of Appeals for the Seventh Circuit,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Court_of_Appeals_for_the_Seventh_Circuit) which includes district courts for the [Eastern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_District_Court_for_the_Eastern_District_of_Wisconsin) [District of Wisconsin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_District_Court_for_the_Eastern_District_of_Wisconsin) and [Western District of Wisconsin.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_District_Court_for_the_Western_District_of_Wisconsin)

## **Politics**

**2024 United States presidential election in Wisconsin**

## ![](_page_13_Picture_2.jpeg)

## ![](_page_13_Picture_3.jpeg)

## Results by county Democratic Republican

## Results by municipality

Wisconsin is considered a [swing state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swing_state) at the federal level, being won by either the [Democratic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Party_(United_States)) or [Republican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republican_Party_(United_States)) nominees[.\[215\]](#page-25-6) Republican [Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump) won the state by 0.58% in the [2024 presidential election;](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_States_presidential_election_in_Wisconsin) it had the closest margin of any state in that election. Democrat [Joe Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Biden) won the state by a narrow margin of 0.63% in [2020;](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_United_States_presidential_election_in_Wisconsin)[\[216\]](#page-25-7) Trump won Wisconsin in [2016](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_United_States_presidential_election_in_Wisconsin) by a similarly narrow margin of 0.77%, the first time the state voted for a Republican presidential nominee since [1984.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1984_United_States_presidential_election) Wisconsin was part of the [blue wall,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_wall_(U. S._politics)) a group of states the Democratic Party won in each presidential election from [1992](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1992_United_States_presidential_election) to [2012.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_United_States_presidential_election)[\[217\]](#page-25-8)

At the statewide level, Wisconsin is competitive, with control regularly alternating between the two parties. Following the [2014 general elections,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Wisconsin_elections) the governor, lieutenant governor, attorney general, and treasurer were all Republicans, while the secretary of state was a Democrat.[\[218\]](#page-25-9) However, in [2018,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Wisconsin_elections) Democrats won all constitutional statewide offices on the ballot, the first time this happened in Wisconsin since 1982[.\[219\]](#page-25-10)

In a 2020 study, Wisconsin was ranked as the 25th easiest state for citizens to vote in.[\[220\]](#page-25-11) Some have argued the state has experienced [democratic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_backsliding) [backsliding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_backsliding) since 2011.[\[221\]](#page-25-12)[\[222\]](#page-25-13)[\[223\]](#page-25-14) Some political scientists classify Wisconsin as a [hybrid regime;](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hybrid_regime)[\[221\]](#page-25-12) the state's [House of Representative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_House_of_Representatives) and [legislature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_legislature_(United_States)) elections are considered to be [free but not fair,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_and_fair_election) with districts undergoing "extreme [partisan gerrymanders"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerrymandering) to entrench Republicans "beyond [electoral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elections_in_the_United_States) [rotation"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elections_in_the_United_States).[\[221\]](#page-25-12)[\[222\]](#page-25-13)[\[224\]](#page-25-15) The Wisconsin Supreme Court overturned the legislative gerrymander in the 2023 ruling of *[Clarke v. Wisconsin Elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clarke_v._Wisconsin_Elections_Commission) [Commission.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clarke_v._Wisconsin_Elections_Commission)*[\[225\]](#page-25-16)

## ![](_page_13_Picture_9.jpeg)

The [2011 Wisconsin Act 10](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Wisconsin_Act_10) led to large protests around the state capitol building in Madison.[\[226\]](#page-25-5)

During the [American Civil War,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Civil_War) Wisconsin was a [Republican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_United_States_Republican_Party) state; in fact, it is the state that gave birth to the Republican Party, although ethno-religious issues in the late 19th century caused a brief split in the coalition. The [Bennett Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bennett_Law) campaign of 1890 dealt with foreign language teaching in schools. Many Germans switched to the [Democratic Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Party_(United_States)) because of the [Republican Party's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republican_Party_(United_States)) support of the law[.\[227\]](#page-25-17)

Wisconsin's political history is broad in scope, encompassing [Robert La Follette](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_La_Follette) and the [Progressive movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progressive_movement) to prominent anti-communist [Joseph Mc Carthy.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Mc Carthy) From the early 20th century, the [Socialist Party of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_Party_of_America) had a base in Milwaukee. The phenomenon was referred to as "[sewer socialism"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sewer_socialism) because the elected officials were more concerned with public works and reform than with revolution (although revolutionary socialism existed in the city as well). Its influence faded in the late 1950s largely because of the [red scare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Red_Scare) and racial tensions.[\[228\]](#page-25-18) The first socialist mayor of a large city in the United States was [Emil Seidel,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emil_Seidel) elected mayor of Milwaukee in 1910; the city elected three more socialist mayors in [Daniel Hoan,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Hoan) [Frank P. Zeidler,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_P._Zeidler) and [Henry Maier.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Maier) Socialist [newspaper editor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newspaper_editor)

[Victor Berger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor_Berger) was repeatedly elected as a U. S. Representative.

Through the first half of the 20th century, Wisconsin's politics were dominated by Robert La Follette and his sons, originally of the Republican Party, but later of the revived [Progressive Party.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progressive_Party_(United_States,_1924)) Since 1945, the state has maintained a close balance between Republicans and [Democrats.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Party_of_Wisconsin)[\[229\]](#page-25-19) In 2012, Republican presidential nominee [Mitt Romney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mitt_Romney) chose Congressman [Paul Ryan,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Ryan) a [Janesville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janesville,_Wisconsin) native, as his running mate against incumbent President [Barack Obama.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama)

## **International relations**

Wisconsin has sister-state relationships with [Hesse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hesse) in Germany, [Chiba Prefecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chiba_Prefecture) in Japan, [Jalisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jalisco) in Mexico, [Heilongjiang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heilongjiang) in China, and [Nicaragua.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicaragua)[\[230\]](#page-26-0) A [Mexican consulate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_consulate) opened in Milwaukee on July 1, 2016[.\[231\]](#page-26-1)

## **Education**

Wisconsin, along with Michigan and Minnesota, was among the [Midwestern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midwestern) leaders in the emergent American state university movement following the [American Civil War.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Civil_War) By the start of the 20th century, education in the state advocated the ["Wisconsin Idea"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_Idea), which emphasized service to the people of the state. The "Wisconsin Idea" exemplified the Progressive movement within colleges and universities at the time[.\[232\]](#page-26-2)

The [Wisconsin Department of Public Instruction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_Department_of_Public_Instruction) is the [state education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_education_agency) and public library management agency in the state[.\[233\]](#page-26-3) The department is led by the [State Superintendent of Public Instruction,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superintendent_of_Public_Instruction_of_Wisconsin) a non-partisan, constitutional officer elected every four years in the spring primary, six months after the previous year's presidential election.

Public [post-secondary education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post-secondary_education) in Wisconsin consists of two organizations. The [University of Wisconsin System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Wisconsin_System) comprises the state's two tier 1 [research universities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Research_universities) as classified by the [Carnegie Foundation:](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carnegie_Foundation_for_the_Advancement_of_Teaching) the flagship [University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Wisconsin%E2%80%93Madison) [of Wisconsin–Madison,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Wisconsin%E2%80%93Madison) and the [University of Wisconsin–Milwaukee.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Wisconsin%E2%80%93Milwaukee)[\[234\]](#page-26-4) The system has eleven other comprehensive universities and ten two-year branch campuses. It is one of the largest public higher education systems in the country, enrolling more than 160,000 [students](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_student) each year and employing approximately 41,000 faculty and staff statewide[.\[235\]](#page-26-5) The 16-campus [Wisconsin Technical College System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_Technical_College_System) awards two-year associate degrees, one- and two-year technical diplomas, and short-term technical diplomas and certificates. It also provides training and technical assistance to Wisconsin's business and industry community.

[Carroll University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carroll_University) in Waukesha was the first institution of higher education in the state, chartered by the territorial legislature on January 31, 1846, but temporarily suspend operations during the Civil War.[\[236\]](#page-26-6) [Beloit College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beloit_College) is the state's oldest continuously operated college, chartered on February 2, 1846.[\[237\]](#page-26-7) [Marquette University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marquette_University) in Milwaukee is a tier 2 research university and the largest private university in Wisconsin by enrollment[.\[238\]](#page-26-8)[\[239\]](#page-26-9) It became the

## ![](_page_14_Picture_4.jpeg)

The [University of Wisconsin–](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Wisconsin%E2%80%93Madison) [Madison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Wisconsin%E2%80%93Madison) is the flagship campus of the [University of Wisconsin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Wisconsin_System) [System.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Wisconsin_System)

first coeducational Catholic university in the world in 1909.[\[240\]](#page-26-10) Other private colleges and universities include [Alverno College,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alverno_College) [Carthage College,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carthage_College) [Concordia University Wisconsin,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concordia_University_Wisconsin) [Edgewood College,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edgewood_College) [Lakeland College,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lakeland_College_(Wisconsin)) [Lawrence University,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lawrence_University) [Medical College of Wisconsin,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medical_College_of_Wisconsin) [Milwaukee School of](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milwaukee_School_of_Engineering) [Engineering,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milwaukee_School_of_Engineering) [Ripon College,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ripon_College_(Wisconsin)) [St. Norbert College,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Norbert_College) [Wisconsin Lutheran College,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_Lutheran_College) and [Viterbo University.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viterbo_University)

## **Transportation**

Wisconsin is served by eight commercial service airports, in addition to a number of [general aviation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_aviation) airports. [Milwaukee Mitchell International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milwaukee_Mitchell_International_Airport) is the largest airport located in Wisconsin, [categorized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FAA_airport_categories) as a medium-hub primary commercial service facility[.\[241\]](#page-26-11) [Dane County Regional Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dane_County_Regional_Airport) and [Appleton International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Appleton_International_Airport) are classified as small-hub primary commercial airports that service the Madison and Fox Valley areas.

Wisconsin is served by multiple [intercity bus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intercity_bus) operators, which provide service to 71 stops and 53 cities. The following carriers provide scheduled bus service: [Amtrak Thruway,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak_Thruway) [Badger Bus,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Badger_Bus) [Flixbus,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flixbus) [Greyhound Lines,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greyhound_Lines) [Indian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Trails) [Trails,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Trails) Jeff[erson Lines,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jefferson_Lines) [Lamers Bus Lines,](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lamers_Bus_Lines&action=edit&redlink=1) [Megabus,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megabus_(North_America)) [Van Galder Bus Company,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Van_Galder_Bus_Company) and [Wisconsin Coach Lines.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_Coach_Lines)[\[242\]](#page-26-12)

## **Wisconsin passenger rail**

Wisconsin is served by eight [Interstate Highways,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Interstate_Highways_in_Wisconsin) consisting of five [primary routes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_Highway_System#Primary_(one-_and_two-digit)_routes_(contiguous_U. S.)) and three [auxiliary routes.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_Highway_System#Auxiliary_(three-digit)_Interstates_(contiguous_U. S.)) The first part of this system was constructed in 1956, and its most recent expansion took place in 2015, with the addition of [I-41](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_41) to the system. Wisconsin's longest Interstate Highway is [I-94.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_94_in_Wisconsin) There are also fourteen [United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Numbered_Highways)

[Numbered Highways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Numbered_Highways) in the state of Wisconsin, which were designated beginning in 1926 and ending in the mid-1930s. There are also several [business](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Business_route) [routes,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Business_route) usually maintained by local governments. The [Wisconsin Department of Transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_Department_of_Transportation) maintains [158 state trunk highways,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_State_Trunk_Highway_System) ranging from two-lane rural roads to limited-access freeways. These highways are paid for by the state's Transportation Fund, which is considered unique among state highway funds because it is kept entirely separate from the general fund, therefore, revenues received from transportation services are required to be used on transportation. The majority of state highway funding comes from gas taxes and vehicle registration fees[.\[243\]](#page-26-13)

[Amtrak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak) provides daily passenger rail service between Milwaukee and Chicago through the *[Hiawatha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hiawatha_(Amtrak_train))*. The *[Borealis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borealis_(train))* provides daily service to Chicago and [Saint Paul, Minnesota,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Paul,_Minnesota) and is supplemental to the long-distance cross-country *[Empire Builder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_Builder)*, both with stops in several cities across Wisconsin[.\[244\]](#page-26-14) Commuter rail provider [Metra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metra)'s Union Pacifi[c North \(UP-N\) line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_Pacific_/_North_Line) has its northern terminus in [Kenosha,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kenosha) the only Metra line and station in the state of Wisconsin[.\[245\]](#page-26-15) [The](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hop) [Hop,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hop) a modern streetcar system in Milwaukee, began service in 2018. The 2.1 mile (3.4 km) initial line runs from [Milwaukee Intermodal Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milwaukee_Intermodal_Station) to [Burns Commons.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burns_Commons)

## **State symbols**

Wisconsin is traditionally known as the "Badger State" due to its early history in [lead](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lead) [mining.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mining) Many of the state's first settlers were drawn by the prospect of mining in southwest Wisconsin, a mineral-rich region which had been contested between [Native Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_Americans_in_the_United_States) and the U. S. Some of the miners lived burrowed within hillsides either due to lack of time or finances to build above-ground structures during the winter. Similar to the [American badger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_badger) using its claws to dig holes, the miners were nicknamed "badgers". The [University of Wisconsin–Madison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Wisconsin%E2%80%93Madison) adopted the badger as a mascot in 1889 after the nickname; the badger was named Wisconsin's state animal in 1957[.\[246\]](#page-26-16)

▪ [State wildlife animal:](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U. S._state_mammals) [white-tailed deer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White-tailed_deer)

▪ [State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U. S._state_fish) fish: [muskellunge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muskellunge)

▪ [State domestic animal:](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U. S._state_mammals) [dairy cow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cattle)
